# 2025 EMX2t CHAMPION VACLAV KOVAR

**ONE OF THE MOST APPEALING ASPECTS OF MOTOCROSS HAS TO BE THE DIVERSITY OF THE CHARACTERS THAT IT ATTRACTS. THIS IS A SPORT WHERE YOU GET MUDDY, YOU GET HIT BY ROCKS AND DIRT CONSTANTLY, AND INJURIES ARE A COMMON OCCURRENCE, BUT THE ADRENALINE OF RIDING AND THE RUSH OF RACING APPEALS TO A WIDE RANGE OF PERSONALITIES THAT ARE ALL THE MORE INTERESTING WHEN THEY CLASH ON CIRCUIT.**

One such character whose distinctive style grabbed the attention of fans was the new EMX 2T Champion for 2025. The 19<sup>th</sup> -century moustache and bright pink race kit stood out from the crowd, but the joy that shone through was apparent as Czech veteran Vaclav Kovar soaked in the winning feeling on home soil at Loket for the very first time!

Vaclav hails from the town of Pisek in the southern central region of Czechia, known as Bohemia, around 200km from the circuit of Loket. He is not related at all to 1990s GP rider Petr Kovar – _“We just have the same name”_ , he says! Pisek is known for having the oldest bridge in the country, and amazingly it’s the Czech word for sand! Although there aren’t many circuits in the area with that surface, and as Vaclav himself says, “I enjoy sandy tracks, but I ride them slowly!”

|PLACE OF BIRTH|**PISEK, CZECHIA**|
|RACING NUMBER|**101**|

In the mid-2000s he was part of a growing pack of riders from central-eastern Europe that hit the youth scene with serious pace. After starting the sport at the age of six, he won the EMX65 Championship, then a six-round series with most of the races geographically held between Italy and Türkiye, at his first attempt in 2006. The 11-year-old Kovar took five of the first six race wins in the opening rounds of Romania and Slovenia. A certain local by the name of Tim Gajser finished behind Kovar in two of the Slovenian races, and ended up third in the Championship, with another future GP rider Vsevelod Brylyakov taking second overall. Overall victory in Bulgaria set Kovar up for a nice finish to the series, which culminated with three third places at Fermo in Italy to clinch the crown!

Moving up to the 85cc class pitted him against another future MXGP legend, Jeffrey Herlings, and in a much more sandy series the young Czech kid was ninth in the final standings, a position he would maintain for the following two years. He took a single podium result from each season, from races in Romania and Poland, before winning the opening round of the 2010 series in Lithuania, just ahead of eventual Champion Brian Bogers. Kovar earned his best World Juniors finish that year as well, with a fifth overall at the French circuit of Gueugnon, directly behind Pauls Jonass in an event won by German future pro Henry Jacobi, with Gajser second overall.

**195**

## Pagina 196

EMX CHAMPIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

The results weren’t so forthcoming for Kovar in the 125cc class, although he took a good sixth overall at the World Juniors in 2012, on his favourite hardpack surface at Sevlievo in Bulgaria, with Gajser again the overall winner ahead of his future mechanic Simone Furlotti, with Jonass third.

In Kovar’s own words, he tried racing through the EMX250 class but didn’t have enough performance, despite taking two Czech titles in 2013 & ’14. He started to race a lot in Germany and became something of a two-stroke specialist. At the age of 20 he competed in the two-stroke EMX300 round at Loket, “just for fun”, and scored a surprise fourth overall. By 2018 he had raised enough sponsorship to compete in the entire seven-round Championship, climbing the podium four times on his way to fifth in the series.

As the Championship was re-branded as EMX2T in 2019, Kovar improved to fourth in points, with a best of second overall to home hero Brad Anderson at Matterley Basin. After the series got reduced to a single-round Championship at his home venue of Loket, he took third in race one in 2023 but did not finish in race two, then took a solid podium in 2024 with 6-3 results.

Finally, he sealed a second lifetime EMX title, 19 years after the first, with a brilliant 2-1 at Loket to win from Italians Andrea Rossi and Mirko Valsecchi! With his own distinctive style and eye-catching kit that included a baseball look on Saturday, and that bright pink look on a triumphant Sunday, he was a very popular winner among more than just the delighted local fans!

He continues to campaign the two-stroke in National Championship races, where he is “happy for every four-stroke behind me”! He also holds many training schools in his home country and works closely with KTM in the Czech Republic.  For a rider who adopted the number 101 after watching Ricky Carmichael dominate the 2005 Motocross of Nations, it was fantastic to take an EMX gold plate just months before his 30th birthday and proof that old-school chargers with great facial hair still have every chance of success in modern Motocross!

**196**

## Pagina 197

EMX CHAMPIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

# 2025 EMX85 CHAMPION ENZO HERZOGENRATH

**THE WEEKEND IN LOKET, CZECH REPUBLIC, WAS ONE TO REMEMBER FOR ENZO HERZOGENRATH, WHO DELIVERED A DOMINANT AND COMPOSED PERFORMANCE TO CLAIM THE 2025 EMX85 EUROPEAN TITLE. REPRESENTING FRANCE, HERZOGENRATH HANDLED THE PRESSURE OF A SINGLE-ROUND FINAL WITH CONFIDENCE AND INTELLIGENCE, SHOWING MATURITY WELL BEYOND HIS YEARS.**

From the very first race, Herzogenrath made his intentions clear. He grabbed the holeshot and steadily built a lead, riding clean and smooth as the track proved technical and challenging. His smart approach paid off with a first-place finish, setting him up perfectly for the second race.

|**RIDER PROFILE**||
|---|---|
|AGE|**14**|
|NATIONALITY|**FRENCH**|
|RACING NUMBER|**282**|

## **BEST RESULTS**

2025 EMX85 EUROPEAN CHAMPION LEADER IN 24MX TOUR ESPOIRS (85CC) SUPERCROSS (SX) ESPOIR LEADER IN 2025

Race two tested the young Frenchman in difficult, slippery conditions. Starting in second, Herzogenrath patiently mastered the tough Loket track, avoiding mistakes while others struggled in the ruts. A controlled third-place finish was enough to secure the overall win and the European crown.

_“It’s incredible! In the first race I got the holeshot and took the win, which felt amazing. In the second race, I started second and made my way to third on a really difficult track. I was also a bit more stressed and didn’t want to make the same mistake as in the Junior World Championship in Romagné! I’m so happy, I am European Champion, yes!"_ cheered Herzogenrath after race.

**197**

## Pagina 198

EMX CHAMPIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

Beyond the results, Herzogenrath’s performance highlighted his development as a complete rider. His combination of speed, consistency and mental toughness marks him as one of the brightest talents in European youth motocross. Already a standout on the national stage with strong results in his home country, he continues to rise through the ranks as one of France’s most promising young riders.

Off the track, Enzo comes from a supportive family. His father has spoken openly about balancing ambition with safety. Not just chasing results but building a sustainable and responsible career. Enzo would train mid week, balance school and attend specialized motocross camps in the off-season to improve. Despite his young age, Enzo already demonstrates a mature mindset combining strong racing instincts with technical skill.

With his 2025 title, Enzo Herzogenrath has firmly established himself among the top prospects in European motocross. If this performance is anything to go by, his journey through the EMX ranks is only just beginning.

**198**

## Pagina 199

EMX CHAMPIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

# 2025 EMX65 EUROPEAN CHAMPION MATHYS AGULLO

**MATHYS AGULLO WROTE HIS NAME INTO EUROPEAN MOTOCROSS HISTORY WITH A FLAWLESS PERFORMANCE AT THE EMX65 EUROPEAN FINALS IN LOKET. SHOWING MATURITY AND PRECISION WELL BEYOND HIS AGE, THE YOUNG FRENCHMAN DELIVERED TWO NEAR-PERFECT RACES TO SECURE THE 2025 EMX65 EUROPEAN CHAMPION TITLE.**

The weekend began with a statement in Race one. As the gate dropped, Agullo launched into the lead with the Fox Holeshot, immediately placing himself at the front of the pack. The opening laps were intense, filled with pressure from riders behind and the challenges of the Czech hard-pack circuit. Despite a small tip-over early in the race, Agullo quickly recovered, regained control and continued to charge. His pace, focus and ability to stay calm under pressure allowed him to rebuild his advantage and take the first race win.

|**RIDER PROFILE**||
|---|---|
|AGE|**11**|
|NATIONALITY|**FRENCH**|
|RACING NUMBER|**355**|
|**ACHIEVEMENTS**||
|EMX65 EUROPEAN CHAMPION||
|EMX65 WORLD VICE-CHAMPION||

Race two showcased the same determination. After settling into the top positions during the opening lap, Agullo made a decisive pass on Eleu Jose around lap two. Once in the lead, he never looked back. Lap by lap, he extended his advantage and pulled away from the field. When he crossed the finish line, Agullo had secured a dominant 1–1 result with a perfect 50-point score at the European Finals.

**199**

## Pagina 200

EMX CHAMPIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

This double-victory earned Mathys Agullo the 2025 EMX65 European Champion title, marking a major milestone in his racing career. His strong starts and ability to handle pressure were key ingredients in his success at one of the most important youth motocross events of the year.

After the race, Agullo expressed both pride and gratitude, crediting the achievement to consistent riding, solid starts and the people who supported him. _“I had a good weekend, a really good weekend. I rode well, I did what I had to do, I made good starts and I'm really happy to be European Champion, that's what I wanted. It's a shame with the Junior World Champion, but today I'm a European champion, which is great. I want to thank my parents, my dad, my mum, my coach Cyril and all my other sponsors"_ stated Agullo.

With his gold medal performance in Loket, Mathys Agullo has established himself as one of the standout young riders in European motocross. His speed, composure and professionalism signal a promising future and the motocross world will undoubtedly watch his next steps closely.

EMX REGULATIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

# 2026 FIM EMX REGULATIONS

**SHORT VERSION OF THE 2026 FIM-EUROPE CHAMPIONSHIPS: EMX65 / EMX85 / EMX125 / EMX250 / EMX2T / EMX OPEN / WOMEN’S EMX MOTOCROSS EUROPEAN CHAMPIONSHIPS REGULATIONS**

## **GENERAL**

## **CLASSES**

- Individual Motocross European Championships for riders and manufacturers.

- Officials at European Championship events when combined with MXGP/MX2: Event Management, Race Direction, FIM Stewards Panel.

- Officials at events when not combined with MXGP/ MX2: International Jury.

- **A** EMX2t 2-stroke Motocross European Championship. For 2023 this is a one-event championship.

**B** EMX250 Motocross European Championship.

**C** EMX125 2-stroke Motocross European Championship.

**D** EMX Open Motocross European Championship.

**E** EMX85 2-stroke Motocross European Championship.

- All riders must have a CONTINENTAL CHAMPIONSHIP Annual or One-Event licence available to the rider’s Federation via the FIM Extranet. **FIM licenses cannot be accepted. EMX65, EMX85 and WEMX riders can have a Continental Championship Promosport licence, annual or one-event. All other classes must have a full Continental Championship license, annual or one-event. NOTE: Junior Continental licenses are no longer available.**

Riders are responsible for and must rely on their own judgement in the selection of helmets and apparel which will provide appropriate and durable protection.

The helmet, eye protection, equipment and protective clothing must be in accordance with the FIM Technical Rules Motocross of the current year apparel: riders must Riders in EMX65, EMX85 and EMX125 **must** also wear shoulder, elbow and knee protection at all times whilst on track. This is **recommended** for all other classes.

- Any outside assistance on the course to a rider is forbidden unless it is provided by a marshal in the interests of safety. However, marshals are not allowed to hold a rider’s motorcycle or to help a rider restart his motorcycle.

- Riders in EMX65, EMX85, WEMX and in the Women’s Motocross of European Nations may use starting blocks and in all classes where the metal serrated grids are in place at MXGP events.

- It is essential to inform the promoter and/or the organiser of non-attendance at any entered event before midday (local time)  of technical verification. Failure to do so will result in a fine of 300 Euros by FIM-Europe.

**F** EMX65 2-stroke Motocross European Championship.

**G** WEMX championship (Women).

Each rider must present one motorcycle in his name and number at technical control. They may present a second motorcycle which must be of the same make, type and cylinder capacity as the first one.

The EMX65 and EMX85 and OPEN European Championships will be held in 4 zones. For 2026 there will be a maximum of 3 pre rounds in each zone. Max 1 for open class,10 riders going to the FINAL in Lommel.

The top 25 riders of each final classification of each zone will be invited by Infront Moto Racing to compete in the 2 semi-finals. One semi-final is between Central and North Zone, another semi-final will be between SW and SE Zone. The top 20 of each semi-final will be invited to the Finals. The Semi Final between SW and SE Zone will be at 20-21 Juin 2026 in Bulgaria. The Semi Final North and Central Zone will be at 11-12  July 2026 in Estonia. The Final will be held in conjunction with the MXGP/ MX2 World Championship round in Loket on 25 /26 July 2026. Infront Moto Racing can invite a maximum of additional riders for each class to the Finals also for the OPEN class, with a maximum of 50 riders per class. The top 40 plus 2 reserves in the qualifying timed trials will compete in the 2-point races to determine the champions. (This event will host the European EMX65 and EMX85 champions)

The Semi-Finals location and date will be decided by FIM Europe MX Commission. The Finals location and date are by Infront.

### **At the finals, Infront Moto Racing may charge an entry fee/facility fee which will be decided by Infront Moto Racing.**

**License is issued by an FMN from another CONU, the rider must nominate the zone he/she will compete in. Changing of zones is forbidden.**

## **THE 4 ZONES ARE AS FOLLOWS:**

## **The 4 EMX65/EMX85 and OPEN zones are as follows:**

- **North Europe –** Finland, Norway, Sweden, Denmark, Germany, The Netherlands, Belgium, Luxembourg, Great Britain, Ireland, Iceland, Czech Republic and Slovakia.

- **Centre Europe –** Poland, Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, Ukraine, Belarus, Russia and Kazakhstan.

- **South East Europe –** Hungary, Romania, Moldova, Bulgaria, Turkey, Azerbaijan, Bosnia-Herzegovina, Cyprus, Greece, Israel, Macedonia, Montenegro and Serbia.

- **South West Europe –** Andorra, Croatia, Portugal, Spain, France, Italy, Monaco, San Marino, Slovenia, Switzerland, Austria,Israel, Liechtenstein and Malta.

# **FORMAT OF THE EVENT**

**EMX250 / EMX125 2-stroke / EMX2t and EMXOpen**

### **DURATION PER SESSION**

#### **MAXIMUM NUMBER OF RIDERS**

## **Up to 50 riders - top 40 riders qualified + 2 reserves**

One or more qualifying <u>group dependent on the number of riders present</u>

|---|---|---|

Practice||

|**2 X Races**|25 minutes + 2 laps|40 riders|

## **Up to 50 riders present One Group**

The top 40 fastest riders from the Qualifying timed practise plus 2 reserves will qualify for the 2 point scoring championship races.

The first reserve rider will be from the pole position winner’s Qualifying Group (regardless whether from Group “1” or “2”). The second reserve rider will be from the remaining Qualifying Group

## **More than 50 riders present and up to 100 riders present Two Groups**

With reference to the above format, if there are more than 50 riders present and up to 100 riders present for EMX125, EMX250, **EMXOpen** and/or EMX2t, the riders present will be placed in 2 groups.

The composition of these two groups will be done based upon the position of the riders in the current European Championship. The placement of riders who do not appear in the current European Championship as well as for the first event of the current European Championship will be decided by ballot.

In this case, the top 20 from each group in Timed Practice will qualify for the main races The first reserve rider will be from the pole position winner’s Qualifying Group (regardless whether from Group “1” or “2”). The second reserve rider will be from the remaining Qualifying Group.

## **More than 100 riders present and up to 150 riders present Three Groups**

With reference to the above format, if there are more than **100** riders present and up to **150** riders present for EMX125, EMX250, EMX Open and/or EMX2t, the riders present will be placed in 3 groups.

The composition of these **three** groups will be done based upon the position of the riders in the current European Championship. The placement of riders who do not appear in the current European Championship as well as for the first event of the current European Championship will be decided by ballot.

In this case, the top 13 from each group in Qualifying Practice will qualify for the main races.

The last qualifying rider will be the 14th best time among the three groups. The first reserve will be the rider with second best 14th position among the three qualifying groups. The second reserve rider will be the rider with the third best 14th position among the three qualifying groups.

Each rider may bring two motorcycles into the waiting area. The motorcycle that will be used for the sighting lap must be placed in the lower floor of the Skybox. If a second motorcycle is brought, it must be placed in the waiting zone behind or close to the Skybox (both motorcycles must have a transponder).

Riders are not allowed to enter the pit lane during the sighting lap. Adjustments, wheel changes, repairs or refuelling must be done in the waiting zone/Skybox. Entering the pit lane during the sighting lap will be considered as attempting to gain an advantage for which the penalty will be disqualification from the respective race.

If the rider uses a different motorcycle for the sighting lap than for the race, he must place this motorcycle in the waiting zone behind or close to the Skybox. If he uses the same motorcycle for the sighting lap and the race, he proceeds with this motorcycle to the Skybox.

The rider’s team is responsible for taking the second motorcycle (the one that will not be used for the race) back to the paddock immediately after having received the instructions from an official.

EMX REGULATIONS **2026 OFFICIAL GUIDE**

**A rider can change motorcycle, change wheels or remove re-usable tyre covers as long as he has not crossed the rear barrier behind the starting gate with it yet. The penalty for violation of this regulation is disqualification from the race in question. However, by changing motorcycle, wheels or removing re-usable tyre covers, the rider may lose his initial starting position.**

**In case of two qualifying groups the first reserve rider will be from the pole position winner’s Qualifying Group (regardless whether from Group “1”, “2”). The second reserve rider will be from the remaining Qualifying Group.**

# **MOTORCYCLES AND CLASSES**

**A** EMXOpen  motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, **over 175cc up to 500cc for 2-stroke and over 175cc up to 450cc for 4-stroke engines**

- **B** EMX2t motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, over 175cc and up to 250cc for 2-stroke engines

- **C** EMX250 motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, **2-stroke and 4-stroke, over 175cc and up to 250cc D** EMX125 motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, up to 125cc for 2-stroke engines

- **E** EMX85 motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, up to 85cc for 2-stroke engines

**F** EMX65 motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, over 50cc and up to 65cc for 2-stroke engines _Minimum weight 53kg, rear wheel max.12 inch, front wheel max. 14 inch only spoke wheels_

# **AGE LIMITS**

FIM EUROPE Motocross European Championship licenses for riders are issued, only when the rider has attained the minimum age, as indicated below:

||**MINIMUM AGE**|**MAXIMUM AGE**|
|---|---|---|
|Motocross European Championship,EMX250|14years|21|
|Motocross European Championship,EMXOpen|16years|50|
|Motocross European Championship,EMX2t(2 stroke)|15years|50|
|Motocross European Championship,EMX125(2 stroke)|13years|17|
|Motocross European Championship,EMX85(2stroke)|11years|14|
|Motocross European Championship,EMX65(2stroke)|8years|12|
|Women's European Championship|14years 250cc/13years 125|50|
|Women’s Motocross of European Nations|14years 250cc/13years 125|50|
|Motocross of European Nations – EMX2 class rider|14years|21|

**NOTE: In all cases the minimum age must be reached at the time of opening the technical verifications, normally the Friday of the event. The maximum age finishes at the end of the calendar year in which the rider reaches the maximum age limit.**

EMX250: motorcycles of Cat. I, Group A1, over 175cc and up to 250cc for **2-stroke and 4-stroke engines** .

**Women’s EMX: up to 125cc 2-stroke or over 175cc and up to 250cc 2-stroke and 4-stroke.**

The minimum age starts on the date of the rider’s birthday. This should be reached before the start of Technical verifications (normally the Friday of the event).

The maximum age **finishes** at the end of the calendar year in which the rider reaches the age of 12 for EMX65, 14 for EMX85, 16/50 MX OPEN, 17 for EMX125 2-stroke, 21 for EMX250, and **21 for the EMX250 rider in the Motocross of European Nations** and in all other cases reaches the age of 50.

**EMX Open: over 175cc up to 450 4-stroke or over 175cc up to 500cc 2-stroke.**

# **TECHNICAL**

- Riders can use 2 motorcycles during the weekend which have been controlled at technical verification. **In the case of a rider having 2 motorcycles, they must be the same make, type, cubic capacity, etc.**

   - Sound control as per 2014 FIM Technical rules for Motocross.

   - Failure of post-race sound control will be penalised by the loss of 10 positions in the race.

- The final choice of the motorcycle to be used in a race must be made 10 minutes before the start. Riders cannot change motorcycles during a Race.

# **WOMEN’S EMX**

The championship is open to women of a minimum age of 14 years old riding motorcycles from 175cc and up to 250cc 4-stroke and 2-stroke, or 13 years old for 125cc 2-stroke.

## **FORMAT OF THE EVENT**

- **2** races of 20 minutes plus 2 laps plus normal free, timed practice and warm-up.

The event is open to any rider from any Continent, not just Europe.

# **NOTES:  FOR ALL CLASSES AT ALL EVENTS**

**The penalty for violation of the waved yellow and/or medical flags will be the loss of 10 positions from the practise session or race in question, for each violation. Such an action will be considered as a statement of fact to which no protests are possible. This applies to all European Championship classes and events whether non combined events, combined with MXGP. Proposal to reduce penalties for failed sound control from 5 positions to 2 positions for FIM event and from 10 positions to 5 positions for FIM Europe.**

**Whenever several flags are displayed in the same area, the superior flag always takes precedence over the other flag(s).**

**At events combined with MXGP, riders must come to a complete stop if using the goggle lane to change/ replace goggles and/or gloves. Signalling is forbidden from the goggle lane. Signalling to riders can only be done in the pit lane.**
